Avoid using plain text for formulas. Use standard Markdown for headings, lists, and blockquotes.\n\u003e\n\u003e 2.  **Structure and Organization:** A key challenge is that lecture content is often scattered. Your task is to organize it logically.\n\u003e     -   Create dedicated sections for different topics (e.g., \"Tlak in Vzgon,\" \"Redoks Reakcije\").\n\u003e     -   When appropriate, create summary tables for key formulas, clearly listing the **Formula**, its **Description**, and its **Application/Derivation**.\n\u003e\n\u003e 3.  **Focus on \"The Why\" - Not Just \"The How\":** I value deep understanding. Do not just transcribe the solution to a problem.\n\u003e     -   Provide clear, step-by-step derivations for important formulas (e.g., $P \u003d \\rho g h$).\n\u003e     -   Use analogies to explain counter-intuitive concepts (e.g., Pascal\u0027s Principle, atmospheric pressure, injectivity vs. surjectivity).\n\u003e     -   Explain the *reasoning* and *strategy* behind solving a problem.\n\u003e\n\u003e 4.  **Act as a Tutor - Proactive Analysis:** When processing my notes, act as a tutor.\n\u003e     -   **Identify potential mistakes:** If you spot a calculation error or a logical inconsistency in my original notes, perform the correct calculation and present it.\n\u003e     -   **Clarify ambiguity:** If a problem\u0027s wording is confusing (like \"Problem #5\" in Physics), build a detailed physical model, explain the unstated assumptions (\"the system is sealed\"), and justify the lecturer\u0027s intended interpretation. Use \"Critical Analysis\" or \"Note to Self\" sections to highlight these learning points.\n\u003e\n\u003e 5.  **File Management:** For each subject, ask if the new content should be appended to an existing file (e.g., `Fizika.md`, `Kemija.md`) or if a new file should be created.\n\u003e\n\u003e **## User Persona \u0026 Tone:**\n\u003e My learning style is analytical and curious. Covers the second half of the material.\n    *   **Format:** Each partial midterm consists of **5 calculation-based problems**, with each problem worth 2 points (total 10 points).\n    *   **Passing Grade:** A minimum of **50% (5 out of 10 points)** is required to pass.\n    *   **Popravni rok (Retake):** Each partial midterm has **one retake opportunity**, typically scheduled about two weeks after the initial attempt.\n\n*   **Zaključni Kolokvij (Final Midterm):**\n    *   This is for students who **do not pass both** partial midterms.\n    *   **Content:** It covers the **entire semester\u0027s curriculum**.\n    *   **Format:** Consists of **8 calculation-based problems**.\n    *   **Passing Grade:** A minimum of **60%** is required to pass.\n\n\u003e **Note to Self:** The strategy is clear: focus on passing the two partial midterms. The final midterm covers more content and has a higher passing threshold, making it significantly more challenging. You will be assigned to a specific group with a fixed weekly schedule. The schedule will be published on Arnes.\n*   **Prisotnost (Attendance):** Attendance is **100% mandatory**. A4 is not acceptable.                                                     |\n| **Navodila za vaje (Lab Manual)**      | You must bring a printed copy of the lab manual to every session. Working from a phone is not permitted due to safety concerns with chemicals.    |\n\n### Vodenje Laboratorijskega Dnevnika (Keeping a Lab Journal)\n\nA properly maintained lab journal in your A5 notebook is required.\n\n1.  **Številka in Naslov Vaje (Exercise Number and Title):** Clearly label each entry.\n2.  **Datum (Date):** Date of the experiment.\n3.  **Namen Vaje (Purpose):** A brief statement on the goal of the experiment.\n4.  **Meritve (Measurements):** Record all raw data and observations.\n5.  **Izračuni (Calculations):** Show all steps for your calculations.\n6.  **Opis Dela s Komentarjem (Work Description with Commentary):** Describe the procedure in your own words. Log in with your university credentials and search for \"Fakulteta za kemijo in kemijsko tehnologijo\" to find your courses. [[Laboratorijske Vaje (Laboratory Exercises)]]\n*   **Requirement:** All **10 laboratory exercises** must be successfully completed and recognized (`priznane`) by the assistant. This is a non-negotiable prerequisite for proceeding.\n\n### 2. [[Kolokviji (Colloquia)]]\nThere are two pathways to pass the colloquia requirement. The strong recommendation is to follow the continuous learning path with partial colloquia.\n\n#### [[Delni Kolokviji (Partial Colloquia)]]\nThis is the primary method, designed to encourage continuous study.\n*   **Structure:** Two partial colloquia are held during the semester.\n    *   **1st Partial Colloquium:** Held halfway through the lab exercises. It covers the material from the first half of the labs (Exercises 1-5, possibly 1-6 as indicated by the red line in the presentation).\n    *   **2nd Partial Colloquium:** Held after all lab exercises are completed. It covers the material from the second half.\n*   **Format:** Each partial colloquium consists of **5 calculation-based problems**, with each problem valued at 2 points.\n*   **Passing Score:** A score of **50% (5 out of 10 points)** is required to pass each partial colloquium.\n*   **Repetition:** Each partial colloquium has **one** repetition (retake) opportunity.\n*   **Overall Mark:** The final colloquium mark is the average of the two partials, but **both must be passed individually**.\n\n#### [[Zaključni Kolokvij (Final Colloquium)]]\nThis is the alternative for students who do not pass both partial colloquia.\n*   **Condition:** You must have all laboratory exercises passed and recognized to be eligible.\n*   **Format:** Consists of **8 computational problems**, each evaluated with 2 points (16 points total).\n*   **Passing Score:** A score of **60% (9.6 out of 16 points)** is required to pass.\n    *   ***Critical Analysis:*** The document also mentions \"9,5 points\". Since $8 \\times 2 \\times 0.60 \u003d 9.6$, we should assume **9.6 points (effectively 10)** is the target. This path is more difficult due to the higher passing threshold and broader scope of material.\n*   **Schedule:** Held at the end of the winter semester, and again in June and August/September.\n\n### 3. [[Končni Izpit (The Final Exam)]]\n*   **Condition:** Entry is conditional on **passing the colloquium requirement**.\n*   **Passing Score:** A pass result of **51%** is required.\n\n### [[Sestava Končne Ocene (Final Grade Composition)]]\nThe final grade is calculated differently depending on your study programme.\n\n*   **For PROFESSIONAL PROGRAMME STUDENTS – CHEMICAL TECHNOLOGY:**\n    *   `50%` Colloquia Grade\n    *   `50%` Final Exam Grade\n*   **For UNIVERSITY PROGRAMME STUDENTS – CHEMICAL ENGINEERING:**\n    *   `50%` Colloquia Grade\n    *   `50%` Final Exam Grade\n*   **For UNIVERSITY PROGRAMME STUDENTS – CHEMISTRY:**\n    *   `50%` Final Exam (theory from lectures)\n    *   `25%` Assignments from Additional Topics (part of the exam)\n    *   `25%` Colloquia (related to seminar and lab exercises)\n\n\u003e **Note to Self:** It\u0027s crucial to identify my exact study programme and understand the corresponding grading weights. The \"University Programme - Chemistry\" has a significantly different structure, placing less weight on the colloquia.\n\n---\n\n## [[Vodenje Laboratorijskega Dnevnika (Keeping the Lab Journal)]]\n\nThe provided document gives a precise and mandatory structure for your **A5 Format zvezek (A5 Format Notebook)**.\n\n**DNEVNIK**\n**NASLOV VAJE:** \\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\n**Listek št.:** \\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_ (Sheet no. from the assistant)\n\n1.  **NAMEN VAJE** (Purpose of the Exercise)\n2.  **MERITVE** (Measurements)\n    *   **2.1 RAČUNSKI DEL** (Computational Part - copy data from the sheet!)\n    *   **2.2 EKSPERIMENTALNI DEL** (Experimental Part - record your measurements: weights, volumes, densities, etc.!)\n3.  **RAČUNI** (Calculations)\n    *   **3.1 RAČUNSKI DEL**\n    *   **3.2 EKSPERIMENTALNI DEL**\n4.  **OPIS DELA** (Description of Work)\n5.  **REZULTATI** (Results)\n    *   **5.1 RAČUNSKI DEL** (provide ONLY the results!)\n    *   **5.2 EKSPERIMENTALNI DEL** (provide ONLY the results!)\n\n---\n\n## [[Seznam Laboratorijskih Vaj (List of Laboratory Exercises)]]\n\nBased on the lab manual\u0027s table of contents, this is the schedule for the semester:\n\n1.  **Določevanje formule kristalohidrata** (Determination of the Formula of a Crystal Hydrate)\n2.  **Plinski zakoni** (Gas Laws)\n3.  **Raztopine** (Solutions)\n4.  **Topnost** (Solubility)\n5.  **Kisline, baze, soli, nevtralizacijska titracija** (Acids, Bases, Salts, Neutralization Titration)\n6.  **Ionske reakcije, destilacija in retitracija** (Ionic Reactions, Distillation, and Retitration)\n    *   *Note: The red line in the presentation after this exercise likely marks the end of the material for the 1st Partial Colloquium.*\n7.  **Kemijsko ravnotežje, ravnotežna konstanta kemijske reakcije** (Chemical Equilibrium, Equilibrium Constant of a Chemical Reaction)\n8.  **Kemijsko ravnotežje, protolitska ravnotežja v vodnih raztopinah, titracijska krivulja** (Chemical Equilibrium, Protolytic Equilibria in Aqueous Solutions, Titration Curve)\n9.  **Topnostni produkt** (Solubility Product)\n10. **Reakcije oksidacije in redukcije** (Oxidation and Reduction Reactions)\n\n---\n\n## [[Varnost v Laboratoriju (Lab Safety)]]\n\nSafety is the highest priority. The lab manual and presentation provide specific rules. All documents are collected at [FKKT - Komisija za varstvo pri delu](https://www.fkkt.um.si/komisija-za-varstvo-pri-delu/).\n\n### [[Splošna Pravila (General Rules)]]\n*   The laboratory is not a public space. Only authorized personnel are allowed.\n*   You must wear your **lab coat (halja)** at all times.\n*   **Safety glasses (zaščitna očala)** with side protection are mandatory.\n*   Long hair must be tied back.\n*   **Open-toed shoes (sandals) and shorts/skirts are not permitted.**\n*   No eating, drinking, or smoking in the lab.\n*   Always wash your hands after finishing lab work.\n*   Pipetting by mouth is strictly forbidden. Always use a pipette bulb (**propipeta / žogica**).\n\n### [[Ravnanje s Kemikalijami in Opremo (Handling Chemicals and Equipment)]]\n*   Never return excess reagents to the stock bottle.\n*   Dispose of chemical waste in designated, labeled containers, not down the sink.\n*   Work with volatile or hazardous substances in a **fume hood (digestorij)**.\n*   Never place hot objects directly on the lab bench or balance.\n*   Clean any spills immediately according to the assistant\u0027s instructions.\n\n### [[Požarna Varnost (Fire Safety)]]\n*   **Fire Triangle:** Fuel (vnetljiva snov), Oxygen (kisik), Heat (toplota). Firefighting is based on removing one of these components.\n*   **Fire Classes:**\n    *   **A:** Solid combustibles (wood, paper). Use Water, Foam, ABC powder.\n    *   **B:** Flammable liquids (gasoline, oil). Use Foam, ABC/BC powder, CO₂. **Do not use water.**\n    *   **C:** Flammable gases (propane, methane). Use ABC/BC powder.\n    *   **D:** Combustible metals (Mg, Na). Use special Class D powder. **Do not use water.**\n    *   **F:** Cooking oils and fats. Use special Class F extinguishers. **Water will cause an explosion!**\n*   **In Case of Fire:** Stay calm. Use the appropriate extinguisher. For a person on fire, use a fire blanket or shower. Immediately call for help (**112**).\n\n---\n\n## [[Priporočena Literatura (Recommended Literature)]]\n\nFor exercises in stoichiometry, the following sources are recommended:\n\n*   Bukovec, N., et al. (2004). *Vaje iz anorganske kemije, Zbirka nalog*. Ljubljana.\n*   Bukovec, N., et al. (2011). *Osnove kemijskega računanja ZBIRKA NALOG*. Ljubljana.\n*   Drofenik, M. (2013). *Splošna in anorganska kemija*. Maribor, UM FKKT.\n*   Šegedin, P. (1996). *Osnove kemijskega računanja z zbirko nalog*. Ljubljana, UL Biotehniška fakulteta.\n*   Kristl, M., \u0026 Dojer, B. (2015). *Zbirka računskih nalog pri predmetu Kemija*. Maribor, UM FNM.",

14:00.\n*   **Kvizi (Quizzes):** Short quizzes will be held weekly at the beginning of lectures to review content from the previous week.\n\n### [[Vaje (Exercises)]]\n*   **Reševanje nalog pred tablo (Problem solving at the board):** Active participation is expected.\n*   **Tedenska oddaja domačih nalog (Weekly homework submission):** Submitted via the Arnes online classroom.\n\n## [[Izpitni Sistem in Ocenjevanje (Exam System and Grading)]]\n\nThe exam is worth a total of **100 possible points**, split into two main parts:\n*   **Računski del (Computational Part):** 60 points (based on exercises/`vaje`).\n*   **Teoretični del (Theoretical Part):** 40 points (based on lectures/`predavanja`).\n\nThere are two distinct pathways for completing the exam requirement:\n1.  **Sprotni Testi (Continuous Testing):** The recommended path with extra benefits.\n2.  **Klasični Izpitni Roki (Classic Exam Periods):** The standard path with no benefits.\n\n### [[Pogoji za Pozitivno Oceno (Conditions for a Passing Grade)]]\nTo pass the course, you must meet **all** of the following criteria:\n\u003e *   **Skupaj vsaj 50 točk** (A total of at least 50 points).\n\u003e *   Od tega vsaj **15 točk** na teoretičnem delu.\n\u003e *   In vsaj **24 točk** na računskem delu.\n\u003e *   The remaining 11 points (to reach 50) can come from either part.\n\n### [[Sprotni Testi (Continuous Testing Path)]]\nThis path is available only to students enrolled in the first year for the first time (not for those with `podaljšan status`). It offers significant advantages.\n*   **Kvizi (Quizzes):** The average of your best 10 quizzes throughout the semester can add **up to 10 additional points** to your final theoretical score.\n*   **Domače Naloge (Homework):** Successful completion of homework can add **up to 5 additional points** to your final computational score.\n\n\u003e #### **Primer Izračuna Ocene s Sprotnimi Testi (Example Grade Calculation with Continuous Testing)**\n\u003e\n\u003e This example from the lecture shows how the benefits work in practice.\n\u003e\n\u003e | Del (Part)        | 1.T | 2.T | S.T | $\\Sigma$ (Povprečje) | DN | Kvizi | **Skupaj (Total)** | **Pogoj (Condition)** |\n\u003e | ----------------- | --- | --- | --- | :--------------------: | :-: | :---: | :----------------: | :------------------: |\n\u003e | **Računski del**  | 25  | 30  | 14  |     **23**             |  5  |   /   |   **28**           | $\\ge 24 \\checkmark$  |\n\u003e | **Teoretični del** | 10  | 26  | 11  |     **16**             |  /  |   7   |   **23**           | $\\ge 15 \\checkmark$  |\n\u003e |                   |     |     |     |                        |     |       |   **51**           | $\\ge 50 \\checkmark$  |\n\u003e\n\u003e **Step-by-Step Analysis (The \"Why\"):**\n\u003e 1.  **Average Test Scores ($\\Sigma$):** The scores from the three tests (1.T, 2.T, S.T) are averaged.\n\u003e     *   Računski: $(25 + 30 + 14) / 3 \u003d 69 / 3 \u003d 23$ points.\n\u003e     *   Teoretični: $(10 + 26 + 11) / 3 \u003d 47 / 3 \\approx 15.67$, which is rounded to **16** points.\n\u003e 2.  **Adding Bonus Points:** The points from homework (DN) and quizzes are added.\n\u003e     *   Računski: $23 (\\text{tests}) + 5 (\\text{DN}) \u003d 28$ points.\n\u003e     *   Teoretični: $16 (\\text{tests}) + 7 (\\text{Kvizi}) \u003d 23$ points.\n\u003e 3.  **Final Check:** The total scores are checked against the minimum requirements.\n\u003e     *   Računski total (28) is $\\ge 24$. **Pass.**\n\u003e     *   Teoretični total (23) is $\\ge 15$. **Pass.**\n\u003e     *   Overall total ($28 + 23 \u003d 51$) is $\\ge 50$. **Pass.**\n\u003e     *   The student has successfully passed the exam.\n\n### [[Klasičen Izpit (Classic Exam Path)]]\n*   This path has **NOBENIH UGODNOSTI** (no benefits).\n*   Points from quizzes and homework do not count. Your grade is based solely on your performance during the scheduled exam periods.\n\n## [[Logika in Množice (Logic and Sets)]]\n\n*   **Izjavni vezniki (Logical Connectives):**\n    *   Konjunkcija (AND): $P \\land Q$\n    *   Disjunkcija (OR): $P \\lor Q$\n    *   Implikacija (Implies): $P \\Rightarrow Q$\n*   **Negacija implikacije (Negation of Implication):** A key equivalence is that the negation of an implication is *not* another implication.\n    *   $\\neg(P \\Rightarrow Q) \\equiv P \\land \\neg Q$\n    *   **Analogy:** The statement \"If it is raining, then the ground is wet\" is only false in one scenario: \"It is raining AND the ground is NOT wet.\"\n*   **Kartezični produkt (Cartesian Product):** The set of all ordered pairs.\n    *   $A \\times B \u003d \\{ (a, b) | a \\in A \\land b \\in B \\}$\n\n## [[Matematična Indukcija (Mathematical Induction)]]\n\nMathematical induction is a powerful proof technique used to prove that a property $P(n)$ holds for all natural numbers $n$ from a certain starting point.\n\n**The Process:**\n1.  **[[Baza Indukcije (Base Case)]]:** Prove that the statement holds for the initial value, usually $n\u003d1$. This anchors the proof.\n2.  **[[Indukcijski Korak (Inductive Step)]]:**\n    *   **Indukcijska predpostavka (Inductive Hypothesis):** Assume the statement $P(n)$ is true for an arbitrary natural number $n$.\n    *   **Dokaz (Proof):** Using this assumption, prove that the statement must also be true for the next number, $P(n+1)$.\n\n\u003e **Analogy:** Imagine a line of dominoes. The **base case** is tipping over the first domino. The **inductive step** is proving that *if* any given domino falls, it will knock over the *next* one. If both are true, the entire line of dominoes will fall.\n\n#### Primer: Vsota Kvadratov (Example: Sum of Squares)\nDokazati (Prove): $1^2 + 2^2 + \\dots + n^2 \u003d \\frac{n(n+1)(2n+1)}{6}$\n\n1.  **Baza Indukcije ($n\u003d1$):**\n    *   Leva stran (LHS): $1^2 \u003d 1$\n    *   Desna stran (RHS): $\\frac{1(1+1)(2 \\cdot 1+1)}{6} \u003d \\frac{1 \\cdot 2 \\cdot 3}{6} \u003d 1$\n    *   Ker je $LHS \u003d RHS$, baza velja (The base case holds).\n\n2.  **Indukcijski Korak:**\n    *   **Predpostavka (Hypothesis):** Predpostavimo, da velja za $n$:\n        $1^2 + 2^2 + \\dots + n^2 \u003d \\frac{n(n+1)(2n+1)}{6}$\n    *   **Dokažemo za $n+1$ (Prove for $n+1$):**\n        Moramo dokazati (We must prove): $1^2 + \\dots + n^2 + (n+1)^2 \u003d \\frac{(n+1)(n+2)(2n+3)}{6}$\n\n        *   Začnemo z levo stranjo in uporabimo predpostavko:\n        $$(1^2 + 2^2 + \\dots + n^2) + (n+1)^2$$\n        $$\u003d \\frac{n(n+1)(2n+1)}{6} + (n+1)^2$$\n        *   Izpostavimo skupni faktor $(n+1)$:\n        $$\u003d (n+1) \\left[ \\frac{n(2n+1)}{6} + (n+1) \\right]$$\n        $$\u003d (n+1) \\left[ \\frac{2n^2+n+6n+6}{6} \\right]$$\n        $$\u003d (n+1) \\left[ \\frac{2n^2+7n+6}{6} \\right]$$\n        *   Faktoriziramo kvadratni člen $2n^2+7n+6 \u003d (n+2)(2n+3)$:\n        $$\u003d \\frac{(n+1)(n+2)(2n+3)}{6}$$\n        *   This is exactly the right-hand side for $n+1$. The proof is complete.\n\n## [[Realna Števila in Absolutna Vrednost (Real Numbers and Absolute Value)]]\n\n### [[Lastnosti Realnih Števil (Properties of Real Numbers)]]\nThe set of real numbers with addition and multiplication, denoted as $(\\mathbb{R}, +, \\cdot)$, forms a mathematical structure called an **obseg (field)**. This means it satisfies key properties like associativity, commutativity, and has neutral and inverse elements for both operations (except division by zero).\n\n### [[Omejenost Množic (Boundedness of Sets)]]\n*   **Zgornja meja (Upper Bound):** A number $M$ such that all elements in a set are less than or equal to $M$.\n*   **Spodnja meja (Lower Bound):** A number $m$ such that all elements in a set are greater than or equal to $m$.\n*   **Supremum (sup):** The *least* upper bound.\n*   **Infimum (inf):** The *greatest* lower bound.\n*   **Maksimum/Minimum:** If the supremum/infimum is an element of the set itself, it is called the maximum/minimum.\n\n### [[Absolutna Vrednost (Absolute Value)]]\nThe absolute value of a real number $x$ is its distance from zero.\n\n**Definicija (Definition):**\n$|x| \u003d \\begin{cases} x \u0026 \\text{; če } x \\ge 0 \\\\ -x \u0026 \\text{; če } x \u003c 0 \\end{cases}$\n\n**Lastnosti (Properties):**\n1.  $|x|^2 \u003d |x \\cdot x| \u003d x^2$\n2.  $|a \\cdot b| \u003d |a| \\cdot |b|$\n3.  $|a+b| \\le |a|+|b|$ (Trikotniška neenakost / Triangle Inequality)\n4.  $|a-b|$ represents the distance between points $a$ and $b$ on the number line.\n\n## [[Literatura (Literature)]]\n\n### [[Teoretični Del (Theory)]]\n*   Petra Žigert Pleteršek, Matevž Črepnjak, *Matematika A, za študente FKKT UM*, Maribor, 2022.\n    *   Available as an e-book on **DKUM** ([https://dk.um.si](https://dk.um.si)).\n\n### [[Računski Del (Problem Books)]]\n*   Brane Butinar, *Matematika I - Naloge z rešitvami*, FKKT, Maribor, 2004.\n*   Matej Mencinger, *Zbirka nalog iz matematične analize in algebre*, FG, Maribor, 2006.\n*   Dobovišek, Hladnik, Omladič, *Rešene naloge iz Analize I*, DMFA, Ljubljana, 2008.\n*   Drinovec Drnovšek, Strle, *Naloge iz Analize 1 - z odgovori, nasveti in rešitvami*, DMFA, Ljubljana, 2021.",

      "text": "Of course. Let\u0027s dive deeper into your first mathematics lecture. I\u0027ve synthesized the information from the presentation slides and your detailed handwritten notes, focusing on explaining the reasoning, deriving key formulas, and walking through the examples step-by-step.\n\nHere is the new, detailed file for your Obsidian vault.\n\n---\n\n### File: `Matematika_A_Predavanje_1.md`\n\n# [[Predavanje 1: Temeljni Pojmi in Matematična Indukcija]]\n\nThis lecture introduces the fundamental building blocks for Matematika A, including logic, number sets, the principle of mathematical induction, and the properties of real numbers and absolute values.\n\n## [[Logika in Množice (Logic and Sets)]]\n\n### [[Izjavni Vezniki (Logical Connectives)]]\nWe use symbols to represent logical operations:\n*   **Konjunkcija (AND):** $P \\land Q$ (Both must be true)\n*   **Disjunkcija (OR):** $P \\lor Q$ (At least one must be true)\n*   **Implikacija (IMPLIES):** $P \\Rightarrow Q$ (If P, then Q)\n\nA crucial point is the **negacija implikacije (negation of an implication)**. It\u0027s often misunderstood, but your notes clarify it perfectly.\n\n\u003e **Razlaga (The \"Why\"):**\n\u003e The negation of \"If P, then Q\" is **not** another implication. The only way for the statement \"$P \\Rightarrow Q$\" to be false is if the premise $P$ is true, but the conclusion $Q$ is false.\n\u003e\n\u003e $$\\neg(P \\Rightarrow Q) \\equiv P \\land \\neg Q$$\n\u003e\n\u003e **Analogy:** Consider the statement \"If it is raining ($P$), then the ground is wet ($Q$).\" This is only false if \"it is raining ($P$) AND the ground is NOT wet ($\\neg Q$).\"\n\n### [[Kartezični Produkt (Cartesian Product)]]\nThe Cartesian product of two sets, $A$ and $B$, is the set of all possible ordered pairs where the first element comes from $A$ and the second from $B$.\n$$ A \\times B \u003d \\{ (a, b) \\,|\\, a \\in A \\land b \\in B \\} $$\n\n**Primer (Example) from your notes:**\n*   Let $A \u003d \\mathbb{R}$ (the set of all real numbers, the entire x-axis).\n*   Let $B \u003d \\{1, 3\\}$ (a set with two distinct numbers).\n*   The Cartesian product $A \\times B$ is then $\\mathbb{R} \\times \\{1, 3\\}$. Geometrically, this represents two infinite horizontal lines at $y\u003d1$ and $y\u003d3$.\n\nThis concept extends to define the real plane: $\\mathbb{R}^2 \u003d \\mathbb{R} \\times \\mathbb{R} \u003d \\{ (x, y) \\,|\\, x, y \\in \\mathbb{R} \\}$.\n\n## [[Številske Množice (Number Sets)]]\nYour notes show a clear hierarchy of number sets, where each is a subset of the next.\n\n*   **Naravna števila ($\\mathbb{N}$):** $\\{1, 2, 3, \\dots\\}$ - Used for counting.\n*   **Cela števila ($\\mathbb{Z}$):** $\\{\\dots, -2, -1, 0, 1, 2, \\dots\\}$ - Includes natural numbers, their negatives, and zero.\n*   **Racionalna števila ($\\mathbb{Q}$):** Numbers that can be expressed as a fraction $\\frac{a}{b}$, where $a, b \\in \\mathbb{Z}$ and $b \\neq 0$.\n*   **Realna števila ($\\mathbb{R}$):** All rational and irrational numbers (like $\\sqrt{2}$, $\\pi$). They fill the number line completely.\n\nThe relationship is: $\\mathbb{N} \\subset \\mathbb{Z} \\subset \\mathbb{Q} \\subset \\mathbb{R}$.\n\n## [[Matematična Indukcija (Mathematical Induction)]]\nThis is a fundamental proof technique to prove that a statement or formula holds true for all natural numbers (or from a certain natural number onwards).\n\n**The Two-Step Process:**\n1.  **[[Baza Indukcije (Base Case)]]:** Prove the statement is true for the first, smallest value of $n$ (usually $n\u003d1$). This is the \"first domino\".\n2.  **[[Indukcijski Korak (Inductive Step)]]:**\n    *   **Indukcijska Predpostavka (Inductive Hypothesis):** *Assume* the statement is true for an arbitrary natural number $n\u003dk$.\n    *   **Dokaz (Proof):** Use this assumption to prove that the statement must then also be true for the next number, $n\u003dk+1$. This proves that \"if one domino falls, the next one will too.\"\n\nIf both steps are successful, the statement is proven true for all natural numbers $n$ from the base case onward.\n\n### Primer 1: Vsota prvih *n* naravnih števil\n**Trditev (Statement):** Dokaži, da za vsak $n \\in \\mathbb{N}$ velja:\n$$ 1 + 2 + 3 + \\dots + n \u003d \\frac{n(n+1)}{2} $$\n\n*   **Baza indukcije ($n\u003d1$):**\n    *   Leva stran (LHS): $1$\n    *   Desna stran (RHS): $\\frac{1(1+1)}{2} \u003d \\frac{2}{2} \u003d 1$.\n    *   Ker je $1\u003d1$, baza velja.\n\n*   **Indukcijski korak:**\n    *   **Predpostavka (Assume true for n):** $1 + 2 + \\dots + n \u003d \\frac{n(n+1)}{2}$.\n    *   **Dokaži za $n+1$ (Prove for n+1):** We want to show that $1 + 2 + \\dots + n + (n+1) \u003d \\frac{(n+1)(n+2)}{2}$.\n        *   Starting with the left side for $n+1$:\n        $$(1 + 2 + \\dots + n) + (n+1)$$\n        *   We substitute our assumption for the part in the parenthesis:\n        $$\u003d \\frac{n(n+1)}{2} + (n+1)$$\n        *   Now, we find a common denominator and simplify:\n        $$\u003d \\frac{n(n+1) + 2(n+1)}{2}$$\n        *   Factor out the common term $(n+1)$:\n        $$\u003d \\frac{(n+1)(n+2)}{2}$$\n        *   This matches the required right-hand side. The proof is complete.\n\n### Primer 2: Vsota Kvadratov\n**Trditev (Statement):** Dokaži, da za vsak $n \\in \\mathbb{N}$ velja:\n$$ 1^2 + 2^2 + \\dots + n^2 \u003d \\frac{n(n+1)(2n+1)}{6} $$\n\n*   **Baza indukcije ($n\u003d1$):**\n    *   LHS: $1^2 \u003d 1$\n    *   RHS: $\\frac{1(1+1)(2 \\cdot 1 + 1)}{6} \u003d \\frac{1 \\cdot 2 \\cdot 3}{6} \u003d 1$.\n    *   Baza velja.\n\n*   **Indukcijski korak:**\n    *   **Predpostavka:** $1^2 + 2^2 + \\dots + n^2 \u003d \\frac{n(n+1)(2n+1)}{6}$\n    *   **Dokaži za $n+1$:** $1^2 + \\dots + n^2 + (n+1)^2 \u003d \\frac{(n+1)((n+1)+1)(2(n+1)+1)}{6} \u003d \\frac{(n+1)(n+2)(2n+3)}{6}$\n        *   Start with the LHS and apply the hypothesis:\n        $$(1^2 + \\dots + n^2) + (n+1)^2 \u003d \\frac{n(n+1)(2n+1)}{6} + (n+1)^2$$\n        *   Factor out $(n+1)$:\n        $$\u003d (n+1) \\left[ \\frac{n(2n+1)}{6} + (n+1) \\right]$$\n        *   Find a common denominator inside the brackets:\n        $$\u003d (n+1) \\left[ \\frac{2n^2+n+6n+6}{6} \\right] \u003d (n+1) \\left[ \\frac{2n^2+7n+6}{6} \\right]$$\n        *   Factor the quadratic polynomial $2n^2+7n+6 \u003d (n+2)(2n+3)$:\n        $$\u003d \\frac{(n+1)(n+2)(2n+3)}{6}$$\n        *   This is the required RHS for $n+1$. The proof is complete.\n\n## [[Absolutna Vrednost (Absolute Value)]]\n\nThis is a measure of a number\u0027s magnitude, regardless of its sign.\n\n### [[Definicija in Lastnosti (Definition and Properties)]]\n*   **Definicija:** $|x| \u003d \\begin{cases} x \u0026 \\text{if } x \\ge 0 \\\\ -x \u0026 \\text{if } x \u003c 0 \\end{cases}$\n*   **Ključne Lastnosti:**\n    1.  **Produkt:** $|a \\cdot b| \u003d |a| \\cdot |b|$\n    2.  **Trikotniška neenakost:** $|a+b| \\le |a|+|b|$. *This is fundamental in analysis. The name comes from the geometric interpretation that the length of one side of a triangle ($\\vec{a}+\\vec{b}$) cannot be longer than the sum of the lengths of the other two sides ($\\vec{a}$ and $\\vec{b}$).*\n    3.  **Razdalja (Distance):** $|a-b|$ is the distance between $a$ and $b$.\n\n### Dokaz: $|x|^2 \u003d x^2$\nThe lecture provided a simple but important proof by cases.\n*   **Case 1 ($x \\ge 0$):** By definition, $|x| \u003d x$. Therefore, $|x|^2 \u003d x^2$.\n*   **Case 2 ($x \u003c 0$):** By definition, $|x| \u003d -x$. Therefore, $|x|^2 \u003d (-x)^2 \u003d x^2$.\n*   **Zaključek:** Since the equality holds in all cases, the statement $|x|^2 \u003d x^2$ is true for all $x \\in \\mathbb{R}$.\n\n### Primer: Reševanje Neenačbe z Absolutno Vrednostjo\n**Naloga (Problem):** Reši neenačbo $|2x+1| \u003c 3$.\n\n*   **Razlaga (The \"Why\"):** The expression $|A| \u003c B$ means that the distance of A from zero is less than B. This is equivalent to saying A is between $-B$ and $B$. So, we can rewrite the inequality without the absolute value.\n    $$-3 \u003c 2x+1 \u003c 3$$\n\n*   **Reševanje (Solving):** We solve this compound inequality.\n    1.  Subtract 1 from all parts:\n        $$-3 - 1 \u003c 2x \u003c 3 - 1$$\n        $$-4 \u003c 2x \u003c 2$$\n    2.  Divide all parts by 2:\n        $$-2 \u003c x \u003c 1$$\n\n*   **Rešitev (Solution):** The solution set is the open interval $x \\in (-2, 1)$.\n\n---\nThis covers the main points from your first lecture\u0027s notes.       }, {

      }, {
        "text": " \\cdot b| \u003d |a| \\cdot |b|$\n    2.  **Trikotniška neenak"

      }, {

This includes the proof for the number of diagonals in a polygon, a breakdown of the Euler method example, and a solution for the absolute value problem from your handwritten notes.\n\n---\n\n### File: `Matematika_A_DN_Raziskava_1.md`\n\n# [[Domače Naloge in Raziskave (Predavanje 1)]]\n\nThis document contains detailed solutions and explanations for the problems and concepts that were assigned for independent study during the first Mathematics A lecture.\n\n## [[Število Diagonal v n-kotniku (Number of Diagonals in an n-gon)]]\n\nThe lecture presented the formula for the number of diagonals in a convex n-sided polygon ($D_n$) and tasked us with proving it via mathematical induction.\n\n**Trditev (Statement):** The number of diagonals in a convex n-gon is given by the formula:\n$$ D_n \u003d \\frac{n(n-3)}{2} $$\n\n**Dokaz z Indukcijo (Proof by Induction):**\n\n### [[Baza Indukcije (Base Case)]]\nThe simplest polygon is a triangle ($n\u003d3$).\n*   A triangle has **0** diagonals.\n*   Let\u0027s check the formula for $n\u003d3$:\n    $$ D_3 \u003d \\frac{3(3-3)}{2} \u003d \\frac{3 \\cdot 0}{2} \u003d 0 $$\n*   The formula holds for the base case.\n    *   *(Self-check for $n\u003d4$, a quadrilateral): It has 2 diagonals. The formula gives $D_4 \u003d \\frac{4(4-3)}{2} \u003d \\frac{4}{2} \u003d 2$. The formula is consistent.*\n\n### [[Indukcijski Korak (Inductive Step)]]\n1.  **Indukcijska Predpostavka (Inductive Hypothesis):**\n    Assume the formula is true for a polygon with $n$ vertices.\n    $$ D_n \u003d \\frac{n(n-3)}{2} $$\n2.  **Dokaz za n+1 (Proof for n+1):**\n    We must prove that the formula holds for a polygon with $n+1$ vertices. Our goal is to show:\n    $$ D_{n+1} \u003d \\frac{(n+1)((n+1)-3)}{2} \u003d \\frac{(n+1)(n-2)}{2} $$\n\n    **Razlaga (The \"Why\"):** How do we get from an $n$-gon to an $(n+1)$-gon? We add a new vertex. Let\u0027s count how the number of diagonals changes.\n\n    *   **Original Diagonals:** All the $D_n$ diagonals from the original $n$-gon are still present.\n    *   **New Diagonals:** From our new vertex, we can draw a diagonal to every other vertex *except* for itself and its two immediate neighbors. This means we can draw diagonals to $(n+1) - 3 \u003d n-2$ vertices. So, we add **$n-2$ new diagonals**.\n    *   **The Converted Side:** One of the *sides* of the original $n$-gon that connected the two vertices adjacent to our new vertex now becomes an *internal diagonal* of the new $(n+1)$-gon. So, we add **1 more diagonal**.\n\n    Therefore, the total number of diagonals in the new polygon is:\n    $$ D_{n+1} \u003d (\\text{original diagonals}) + (\\text{new diagonals}) + (\\text{converted side}) $$\n    $$ D_{n+1} \u003d D_n + (n-2) + 1 \u003d D_n + n - 1 $$\n\n    Now, we substitute our inductive hypothesis for $D_n$:\n    $$ D_{n+1} \u003d \\frac{n(n-3)}{2} + (n-1) $$\n    Find a common denominator:\n    $$ D_{n+1} \u003d \\frac{n(n-3) + 2(n-1)}{2} \u003d \\frac{n^2 - 3n + 2n - 2}{2} $$\n    $$ D_{n+1} \u003d \\frac{n^2 - n - 2}{2} $$\n    Factor the numerator:\n    $$ D_{n+1} \u003d \\frac{(n+1)(n-2)}{2} $$\n    This is exactly the formula we needed to prove for $n+1$. The inductive step holds.\n\n**Zaključek (Conclusion):** Since the base case and the inductive step are both true, the formula $D_n \u003d \\frac{n(n-3)}{2}$ is proven to be valid for all polygons with $n \\ge 3$.\n\n## [[Raziskava: Eulerjeva Numerična Metoda]]\n\nYour notes mention a homework assignment: \"Raziskovalni napad (skripta Zgled 2.2)\" related to the example $y\u0027 \u003d xy$ with the initial condition $y(0)\u003d1$. This refers to **Euler\u0027s Method** for numerically approximating solutions to differential equations.\n\n### [[Koncept (The \"Why\")]]\nEuler\u0027s method is a way to approximate the solution curve of a differential equation without finding the exact analytical solution. The core idea is simple:\n1.  You know the starting point $(x_0, y_0)$.\n2.  The differential equation $y\u0027 \u003d f(x, y)$ gives you the **slope of the tangent line** at that point.\n3.  You assume that for a very small step $h$, the curve behaves almost like this tangent line.\n4.  You take a small step forward along the tangent line to find the next approximate point $(x_1, y_1)$.\n5.  Repeat the process from the new point.\n\nThis generates a sequence of points that approximates the true solution curve.\n\n### [[Formula]]\nThe iterative formula is derived from the point-slope form of a line:\n$$ y_{n+1} \u003d y_n + h \\cdot f(x_n, y_n) $$\nWhere:\n*   $(x_n, y_n)$ is the current point.\n*   $h$ is the step size.\n*   $f(x_n, y_n)$ is the slope at the current point, calculated from the differential equation.\n\n### [[Reševanje Primera (Solving the Example)]]\n**Naloga (Problem):** Approximate the solution for $y\u0027 \u003d xy$ with initial condition $y(0)\u003d1$ using a step size of $h\u003d0.2$. Let\u0027s calculate the first few points as shown in the textbook example.\n\n*   **Given:** $f(x, y) \u003d xy$, $(x_0, y_0) \u003d (0, 1)$, $h\u003d0.2$.\n\n**Korak 1 (Step 1):**\n*   **Point 0:** $(x_0, y_0) \u003d (0, 1)$\n*   **Calculate next point $(x_1, y_1)$:**\n    $$ y_1 \u003d y_0 + h \\cdot (x_0 \\cdot y_0) \u003d 1 + 0.2 \\cdot (0 \\cdot 1) \u003d 1 + 0 \u003d 1 $$\n    $$ x_1 \u003d x_0 + h \u003d 0 + 0.2 \u003d 0.2 $$\n*   **Point 1:** $(0.2, 1)$\n\n**Korak 2 (Step 2):**\n*   **Point 1:** $(x_1, y_1) \u003d (0.2, 1)$\n*   **Calculate next point $(x_2, y_2)$:**\n    $$ y_2 \u003d y_1 + h \\cdot (x_1 \\cdot y_1) \u003d 1 + 0.2 \\cdot (0.2 \\cdot 1) \u003d 1 + 0.04 \u003d 1.04 $$\n    $$ x_2 \u003d x_1 + h \u003d 0.2 + 0.2 \u003d 0.4 $$\n*   **Point 2:** $(0.4, 1.04)$\n\n**Korak 3 (Step 3):**\n*   **Point 2:** $(x_2, y_2) \u003d (0.4, 1.04)$\n*   **Calculate next point $(x_3, y_3)$:**\n    $$ y_3 \u003d y_2 + h \\cdot (x_2 \\cdot y_2) \u003d 1.04 + 0.2 \\cdot (0.4 \\cdot 1.04) \u003d 1.04 + 0.0832 \u003d 1.1232 $$\n    $$ x_3 \u003d x_2 + h \u003d 0.4 + 0.2 \u003d 0.6 $$\n*   **Point 3:** $(0.6, 1.1232)$\n\nThis iterative process matches the values given in the textbook example. The \"research attack\" was to understand and replicate these steps.\n\n## [[Primer Reševanja: Enačba z Absolutno Vrednostjo]]\n\nYour handwritten notes include the problem of solving $|x^2 - 2x| \u003d 4$.\n\n### [[Koncept (The \"Why\")]]\nThe absolute value function $|A|$ splits a problem into two cases. The equation $|A| \u003d B$ is equivalent to solving two separate equations:\n1.  $A \u003d B$\n2.  $A \u003d -B$\n\nHowever, we must be careful to check which solutions are valid. A more rigorous method is to analyze the sign of the expression *inside* the absolute value.\n\n### [[Reševanje po Korakih (Step-by-Step Solution)]]\n**Naloga (Problem):** Reši enačbo $|x^2 - 2x| \u003d 4$.\n\n**1. Določi Kritične Točke (Determine Critical Points):**\nFirst, find where the expression inside the absolute value equals zero. These points divide the number line into regions where the expression is either positive or negative.\n$$ x^2 - 2x \u003d 0 \\implies x(x-2) \u003d 0 $$\nThe critical points are $x\u003d0$ and $x\u003d2$. This gives us two main cases.\n\n**2. Analiza Primerov (Case Analysis):**\n\n*   **Primer 1: $x^2 - 2x \\ge 0$**\n    This is true when $x \\in (-\\infty, 0] \\cup [2, \\infty)$.\n    In this region, the absolute value does nothing: $|x^2 - 2x| \u003d x^2 - 2x$.\n    The equation becomes:\n    $$ x^2 - 2x \u003d 4 \\implies x^2 - 2x - 4 \u003d 0 $$\n    Using the quadratic formula to solve for $x$:\n    $$ x \u003d \\frac{-b \\pm \\sqrt{b^2 - 4ac}}{2a} \u003d \\frac{2 \\pm \\sqrt{(-2)^2 - 4(1)(-4)}}{2} $$\n    $$ x \u003d \\frac{2 \\pm \\sqrt{4 + 16}}{2} \u003d \\frac{2 \\pm \\sqrt{20}}{2} \u003d \\frac{2 \\pm 2\\sqrt{5}}{2} \u003d 1 \\pm \\sqrt{5} $$\n    We must check if these solutions lie in the domain for this case:\n    *   $x_1 \u003d 1 + \\sqrt{5} \\approx 3.236$. This is in $[2, \\infty)$, so it is a **valid solution**.\n    *   $x_2 \u003d 1 - \\sqrt{5} \\approx -1.236$. This is in $(-\\infty, 0]$, so it is also a **valid solution**.\n\n*   **Primer 2: $x^2 - 2x \u003c 0$**\n    This is true when $x \\in (0, 2)$.\n    In this region, the absolute value changes the sign: $|x^2 - 2x| \u003d -(x^2 - 2x) \u003d -x^2 + 2x$.\n    The equation becomes:\n    $$ -x^2 + 2x \u003d 4 \\implies x^2 - 2x + 4 \u003d 0 $$\n    Let\u0027s check the discriminant, $D \u003d b^2 - 4ac$:\n    $$ D \u003d (-2)^2 - 4(1)(4) \u003d 4 - 16 \u003d -12 $$\n    Since the discriminant is negative, there are **no real solutions** in this case.\n\n**3. Končna Rešitev (Final Solution):**\nCombining the results from both cases, the set of solutions is:\n$$ \\{ 1 - \\sqrt{5}, \\, 1 + \\sqrt{5} \\} $$ 1 + \\sqrt{5} \\} $$",

If you take any two elements from the set, and perform the operation, the result *must* still be in the same set. Your notes provide a perfect illustration:\n\n*   **Primer a) Seštevanje sodih števil (Adding even numbers):**\n    Let $S$ be the set of even numbers. If you add any two even numbers, is the result always even? Yes.\n    `even + even \u003d even`\n    Therefore, addition **is** a binary operation on the set of even numbers.\n\n*   **Primer b) Seštevanje lihih števil (Adding odd numbers):**\n    Let $L$ be the set of odd numbers. If you add any two odd numbers, is the result always odd? No.\n    `odd + odd \u003d even`\n    Since the result (an even number) is not in the original set $L$, addition **is not** a binary operation on the set of odd numbers.\n\n### [[Dve Binarni Operaciji na $\\mathbb{R}$ (Two Binary Operations on $\\mathbb{R}$)]]\nFor the set of real numbers, $\\mathbb{R}$, we focus on two main binary operations:\n*   **Seštevanje (Addition):** $(a, b) \\mapsto a+b \\in \\mathbb{R}$\n*   **Množenje (Multiplication):** $(a, b) \\mapsto a \\cdot b \\in \\mathbb{R}$\n\nThese operations, along with their properties, give the real numbers a special structure called an **obseg (field)**.\n\n### [[Lastnosti, ki Določajo Obseg (Properties that Define a Field)]]\n1.  **Lastnosti Seštevanja (Properties of Addition)**\n    *   **Asociativnost (Associativity):** $(a+b)+c \u003d a+(b+c)$\n    *   **Komutativnost (Commutativity):** $a+b\u003db+a$\n    *   **Nevtralni element (Neutral Element):** $a+0 \u003d a$ (The identity for addition is 0)\n    *   **Nasprotni element (Inverse Element):** $a+(-a) \u003d 0$ (Every number has an additive inverse)\n\n2.  **Lastnosti Množenja (Properties of Multiplication)**\n    *   **Asociativnost (Associativity):** $(ab)c \u003d a(bc)$\n    *   **Komutativnost (Commutativity):** $ab \u003d ba$\n    *   **Nevtralni element (Neutral Element):** $a \\cdot 1 \u003d a$ (The identity for multiplication is 1)\n    *   **Inverzni oz. obratni element (Inverse Element):** $a \\cdot a^{-1} \u003d 1$ (Every non-zero number has a multiplicative inverse, e.g., $a \\cdot \\frac{1}{a} \u003d 1$)\n\n3.  **Skupna Lastnost (Shared Property)**\n    *   **Distributivnost (Distributivity):** $a(b+c) \u003d ab+ac$ (Connects multiplication and addition)\n\nBecause $(\\mathbb{R}, +, \\cdot)$ satisfies all these rules, we call it an **obseg (field)**. Subtraction and division are simply inverse operations of this structure:\n*   **Odštevanje (Subtraction):** $a - b$ is defined as $a + (-b)$.\n*   **Deljenje (Division):** $a \\div b$ is defined as $a \\cdot b^{-1}$ (where $b \\neq 0$).\n\n## [[Urejenost Realnih Števil in Podmnožice (Ordering and Subsets of Real Numbers)]]\n\nReal numbers are ordered, which allows us to define intervals. Your notes list the standard types:\n*   **Odprti interval (Open interval):** $(a, b) \u003d \\{ x \\in \\mathbb{R} \\,|\\, a \u003c x \u003c b \\}$\n*   **Zaprti interval (Closed interval):** $[a, b] \u003d \\{ x \\in \\mathbb{R} \\,|\\, a \\le x \\le b \\}$\n*   **Polodprti interval (Half-open interval):** $(a, b]$ or $[a, b)$\n*   **Neskončni intervali (Infinite intervals):** e.g., $[a, \\infty)$\n\n### DN: [[Potenciranje in Korenjenje (Exponentiation and Roots)]]\nYour notes highlight the identity: $\\sqrt[n]{a^m} \u003d a^{\\frac{m}{n}}$.\n\n**Razlaga (The \"Why\"):** This identity comes directly from the definitions of roots and fractional exponents.\n1.  The definition of a root is: $\\sqrt[n]{x} \u003d x^{\\frac{1}{n}}$.\n2.  The power-of-a-power rule for exponents is: $(x^m)^k \u003d x^{m \\cdot k}$.\n\nBy combining these, we can derive the identity:\n$$ \\sqrt[n]{a^m} \u003d (a^m)^{\\frac{1}{n}} \u003d a^{m \\cdot \\frac{1}{n}} \u003d a^{\\frac{m}{n}} $$\n\n## [[Omejenost Množic (Boundedness of Sets)]]\nFor any subset $A \\subseteq \\mathbb{R}$:\n*   $A$ is **navzgor omejena (bounded above)** if there exists an upper bound $M$ such that $x \\le M$ for all $x \\in A$.\n*   $A$ is **navzdol omejena (bounded below)** if there exists a lower bound $m$ such that $x \\ge m$ for all $x \\in A$.\n\n### [[Supremum in Infimum]]\n*   **Supremum (sup A):** The **least upper bound** (`najmanjša zgornja meja`). It is the \"lowest ceiling\" for the set.\n*   **Infimum (inf A):** The **greatest lower bound** (`največja spodnja meja`). It is the \"highest floor\" for the set.\n*   **Maksimum (max A) / Minimum (min A):** If the supremum or infimum is an element *within* the set $A$, it is called the maximum or minimum, respectively.\n\n### Primer: $A \u003d \\{ \\frac{n}{n-1} \\,|\\, n \\in \\mathbb{N}, n \\ge 2 \\}$\nThis is a fantastic example from your notes to illustrate these concepts.\n\n**1.       }, {

      }, {

        "text": "div b$ is defined as $a \\cdot b^{-1}$ (where $b \\neq 0$).\n\n"
      }, {
        "text": "## [[Urejenost Realnih Števil in Podmnožice (Ordering and Subsets of Real Numbers)]]"
      }, {
        "text": "\n\nReal numbers are ordered, which allows us to define intervals. Your notes list the standard types:\n*   **Od"
      }, {
        "text": "prti interval (Open interval):** $(a, b) \u003d \\{ x \\in \\mathbb{R} \\,"

        "text": " interval):** $(a, b]$ or $[a, b)$\n*   **Neskončni intervali (Infinite intervals):** e.g., $[a, \\infty)$\n\n### DN: [[Potenciranje in Korenjenje (Exponentiation and Roots)]]\nYour notes highlight the identity: $\\sqrt[n]{a^m} \u003d a^{\\frac{m}{n}}$.\n\n**Razlaga (The \"Why\"):** This identity comes directly from the definitions of roots and fractional exponents.\n1.  The definition of a root is: $\\sqrt[n]{x} \u003d x^{\\frac{1}{n}}$.\n2.  The power-of-a-power rule for exponents is: $(x^m)^k \u003d x^{m \\cdot k}$.\n\nBy combining these, we can derive the identity:\n$$ \\sqrt[n]{a^m} \u003d (a^m)^{\\frac{1}{n}} \u003d a^{m \\cdot \\frac{1}{n}} \u003d a^{\\frac{m}{n}} $$\n\n## [[Omejenost Množic (Boundedness of Sets)]]\nFor any subset $A \\subseteq \\mathbb{R}$:\n*   $A$ is **navzgor omejena (bounded above)** if there exists an upper bound $M$ such that $x \\le M$ for all $x \\in A$.\n*   $A$ is **navzdol omejena (bounded below)** if there exists a lower bound $m$ such that $x \\ge m$ for all $x \\in A$.\n\n### [[Supremum in Infimum]]\n*   **Supremum (sup A):** The **least upper bound** (`najmanjša zgornja meja`). It is the \"lowest ceiling\" for the set.\n*   **Infimum (inf A):** The **greatest lower bound** (`največja spodnja meja`). It is the \"highest floor\" for the set.\n*   **Maksimum (max A) / Minimum (min A):** If the supremum or infimum is an element *within* the set $A$, it is called the maximum or minimum, respectively.\n\n### Primer: $A \u003d \\{ \\frac{n}{n-1} \\,|\\, n \\in \\mathbb{N}, n \\ge 2 \\}$\nThis is a fantastic example from your notes to illustrate these concepts.\n\n**1. Analiza Množice (Analyzing the Set):**\nLet\u0027s write out the first few elements:\n*   For $n\u003d2$: $\\frac{2}{2-1} \u003d 2$\n*   For $n\u003d3$: $\\frac{3}{3-1} \u003d \\frac{3}{2} \u003d 1.5$\n*   For $n\u003d4$: $\\frac{4}{4-1} \u003d \\frac{4}{3} \\approx 1.33$\n*   For $n\u003d5$: $\\frac{5}{5-1} \u003d \\frac{5}{4} \u003d 1.25$\n$$ A \u003d \\left\\{ 2, \\frac{3}{2}, \\frac{4}{3}, \\frac{5}{4}, \\dots \\right\\} $$\nWe can see the sequence is decreasing and seems to be approaching 1.\n\n**2. Določanje Meja (Determining the Bounds):**\n*   The largest element occurs at $n\u003d2$, which is $2$. All other elements are smaller. Thus, **sup A \u003d 2**. Since $2 \\in A$, we also have **max A \u003d 2**.\n*   The elements are always greater than 1. Let\u0027s prove that **inf A \u003d 1**.\n\n**3. Dokaz, da je inf A \u003d 1 (Proof that inf A \u003d 1):**\nTo prove that 1 is the greatest lower bound, we must show that for any arbitrarily small number $\\epsilon \u003e 0$, we can always find an element of $A$ that is between $1$ and $1+\\epsilon$.\n*   **Goal:** Find an $x \\in A$ such that $1 \u003c x \u003c 1+\\epsilon$.\n*   Substitute $x \u003d \\frac{n}{n-1}$:\n    $$ \\frac{n}{n-1} \u003c 1+\\epsilon $$\n*   Since $n \\ge 2$, $n-1$ is positive, so we can multiply without changing the inequality direction:\n    $$ n \u003c (1+\\epsilon)(n-1) \u003d n - 1 + \\epsilon n - \\epsilon $$\n    $$ 0 \u003c -1 + \\epsilon n - \\epsilon $$\n    $$ 1+\\epsilon \u003c \\epsilon n $$\n    $$ \\frac{1+\\epsilon}{\\epsilon} \u003c n $$\n*   **Zaključek:** This shows that if we choose a natural number $n$ that is larger than $\\frac{1+\\epsilon}{\\epsilon}$, the corresponding element $\\frac{n}{n-1}$ will be in the interval $(1, 1+\\epsilon)$. Since we can do this for *any* $\\epsilon\u003e0$, no matter how small, there can be no lower bound greater than 1. Therefore, **inf A \u003d 1**.\n*   Since $1 \\notin A$ (as shown by the check `1 \u003d n/(n-1)` leading to `-1 \u003d 0`), the set has **no minimum**."

      "text": "That is a fantastic question. It hits on the single most important—and often most confusing—concept in introductory analysis. The idea of epsilon ($\\epsilon$) is the engine that drives proofs about limits, continuity, and, in this case, infimum/supremum.\n\nLet\u0027s break it down using an analogy and then connect it directly to your problem.\n\n### The Core Idea: The \"Wall\" Analogy\n\nImagine the numbers in your set $A \u003d \\{ 2, \\frac{3}{2}, \\frac{4}{3}, \\dots \\}$ as people walking on the number line.\n\n*   They all start somewhere (at 2, 1.5, etc.) and are walking towards the number 1 from the right.\n*   The number 1 is like a solid wall. They get closer and closer to it, but they **never touch it or cross it**.\n\nWe can easily prove that 1 is a \"wall\" (a lower bound). But the question for the **infimum** is:\n\n\u003e Is it the **last possible wall**? Could we have built another wall slightly to the right of 1, say at 1.000001, that all our people also stay to the right of?\n\nIf we can build a wall *even slightly* to the right of 1, then 1 isn\u0027t the *greatest* lower bound.\n\n### The Formal Two-Step Proof of an Infimum\n\nTo formally prove that a number $m$ is the infimum of a set $S$, you must prove **two things**:\n\n1.  **$m$ is a lower bound:** You must show that for every element $x$ in the set $S$, $x \\ge m$. (This is the easy part).\n2.  **$m$ is the *greatest* lower bound:** You must show that there is **NO** number greater than $m$ that is also a lower bound. (This is the hard part, and this is **epsilon\u0027s job**).\n\n### The Role of Epsilon ($\\epsilon$) - The \"Challenger\"\n\nThink of $\\epsilon$ as a **challenge**. It represents an arbitrarily small, positive number.\n\n1.  You make the claim: \"**1 is the greatest lower bound!**\"\n2.  A skeptic comes along and says: \"I don\u0027t believe you. I think there\u0027s a better one, a wall slightly to the right of yours. I propose a new wall at **$1 + \\epsilon$**.\"\n3.  Your job is to prove the skeptic wrong. To do this, you must show that their proposed wall, $1+\\epsilon$, **fails**. You need to find at least one element of your set, let\u0027s call it $x_n$, that has \"snuck past\" their wall. You must find an $x_n$ from your set such that:\n    $$ x_n \u003c 1 + \\epsilon $$\n\nIf you can provide a recipe that finds such an element for **ANY** tiny $\\epsilon$ the skeptic throws at you (no matter how ridiculously small, like 0.1, 0.0001, or $10^{-100}$), then you have proven that **no wall to the right of 1 can possibly work**.\n\nTherefore, 1 must be the greatest lower bound.\n\n### Applying the Epsilon Challenge to Your Problem\n\nLet\u0027s walk through the proof from your notes with this new \"challenger\" mindset.\n\n**Our Goal:** Prove that 1 is the infimum of $A \u003d \\{ \\frac{n}{n-1} \\,|\\, n \\ge 2 \\}$.\n\n**Step 1: Prove 1 is a lower bound.**\nWe want to show $\\frac{n}{n-1} \\ge 1$ for all $n \\ge 2$.\n$$ \\frac{n}{n-1} \u003d \\frac{(n-1)+1}{n-1} \u003d \\frac{n-1}{n-1} + \\frac{1}{n-1} \u003d 1 + \\frac{1}{n-1} $$\nSince $n \\ge 2$, the term $\\frac{1}{n-1}$ is always positive. Therefore, $1 + \\frac{1}{n-1}$ is always greater than 1.\n**Conclusion:** 1 is a lower bound. (Easy part done).\n\n**Step 2: Prove 1 is the *greatest* lower bound.**\nThe skeptic challenges us with a new potential lower bound: $1 + \\epsilon$.\nWe must prove them wrong by finding an element $\\frac{n}{n-1}$ from our set that is *smaller* than their proposed bound.\n\n**The Challenge:** Find an $n$ such that:\n$$ \\frac{n}{n-1} \u003c 1 + \\epsilon $$\n\nNow we just solve for $n$. This algebra is the \"recipe\" to defeat the skeptic.\n$$ n \u003c (1+\\epsilon)(n-1) $$\n$$ n \u003c n - 1 + \\epsilon n - \\epsilon $$\nSubtract $n$ from both sides:\n$$ 0 \u003c -1 + \\epsilon n - \\epsilon $$\nRearrange the terms to isolate $n$:\n$$ 1 + \\epsilon \u003c \\epsilon n $$\n$$ \\frac{1+\\epsilon}{\\epsilon} \u003c n $$\n\n**This is our victory condition!** This formula tells us how to win.\n\n*   **Skeptic challenges with $\\epsilon \u003d 0.1$:**\n    Our formula says we need to find an $n \u003e \\frac{1+0.1}{0.1} \u003d \\frac{1.1}{0.1} \u003d 11$.\n    Let\u0027s pick $n\u003d12$. The element is $\\frac{12}{11} \\approx 1.0909$.\n    Is our element smaller than the skeptic\u0027s wall? Is $1.0909 \u003c 1 + 0.1$? Yes, $1.0909 \u003c 1.1$.\n    We found an element that is \"past their wall,\" so their wall fails. We win this round.\n\n*   **Skeptic gets serious with $\\epsilon \u003d 0.001$ (a wall at 1.001):**\n    Our formula says we need $n \u003e \\frac{1+0.001}{0.001} \u003d 1001$.\n    Let\u0027s pick $n\u003d1002$. The element is $\\frac{1002}{1001} \\approx 1.000999$.\n    Is $1.000999 \u003c 1.001$? Yes. We win again.\n\n### Summary\n\n**The importance of epsilon is that it formalizes the concept of \"arbitrarily close\".**\n\nBy showing that for **ANY** epsilon greater than zero, you can always find an element of the set in the gap between $1$ and $1+\\epsilon$, you prove that there is no \"empty space\" where a better lower bound could exist. This is the rigorous way of saying the elements of the set \"squeeze up\" right against the number 1, even if they never touch it.",

To potrjuje, da je intuicija pravilna in mi daje osnovo za bazo indukcije.\n\nPoskusil sem tudi poenostaviti problem, da bi videl, če drži. Če bi predpostavil, da so vsi $a_i$ enaki (npr. $a_i \u003d a$ za vse $i$), bi neenakost postala:\n$$(1+a)^n \u003e 1 + na$$\nTo je znana Bernoullijeva neenakost, ki drži. Čeprav to ni dokaz za naš splošni primer (ker $a_i$ niso nujno enaki), mi to daje samozavest, da je trditev verjetno resnična.\n\nFormalno bom trditev dokazal z metodo matematične indukcije, saj velja za naravna števila $n \\ge 2$. Sledil bom standardnemu postopku:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n    \\item \\textbf{Baza indukcije:} Dokazal bom, da trditev velja za najmanjši možni $n$, torej $n\u003d2$.\n    \\item \\textbf{Indukcijski korak:} Predpostavil bom, da neenakost velja za nek poljuben $n\u003dk$ (indukcijska hipoteza) in nato s pomočjo te predpostavke dokazal, da mora veljati tudi za naslednji člen, $n\u003dk+1$.\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\section*{Formalni dokaz z matematično indukcijo}\n\n\\subsection*{1. Baza indukcije ($n\u003d2$)}\nPreverimo, ali neenakost velja za $n\u003d2$.\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item Leva stran (LHS): $(1+a_1)(1+a_2) \u003d 1 + a_1 + a_2 + a_1a_2$.\n    \\item Desna stran (RHS): $1+a_1+a_2$.\n\\end{itemize}\nPrimerjamo obe strani:\n$$ 1 + a_1 + a_2 + a_1a_2 \u003e 1+a_1+a_2 $$\nTa neenakost je resnična, če in samo če velja $a_1a_2 \u003e 0$.\n\\textbf{Utemeljitev:} Ker je po definiciji $a_i \\in \\mathbb{R}^+$ za vsak $i$, pomeni, da sta $a_1 \u003e 0$ in $a_2 \u003e 0$. Produkt dveh pozitivnih števil je vedno pozitiven, torej je $a_1a_2 \u003e 0$.\nS tem je baza indukcije dokazana.\n\n\\subsection*{2. Indukcijski korak}\n\\textbf{Indukcijska predpostavka (hipoteza):} Predpostavimo, da neenakost velja za nek poljuben naraven $k \\ge 2$:\n$$ (1+a_1)(1+a_2)\\dots(1+a_k) \u003e 1+a_1+a_2+\\dots+a_k $$\n\\textbf{Dokaz za $n\u003dk+1$:} Dokazati moramo, da iz predpostavke sledi veljavnost neenakosti za $n\u003dk+1$:\n$$ (1+a_1)(1+a_2)\\dots(1+a_k)(1+a_{k+1}) \u003e 1+a_1+a_2+\\dots+a_k+a_{k+1} $$\nZačnimo z levo stranjo neenakosti za $n\u003dk+1$ in jo preoblikujmo:\n$$ \\text{LHS} \u003d \\underbrace{(1+a_1)(1+a_2)\\dots(1+a_k)}_{\\text{Uporabimo indukcijsko predpostavko}} (1+a_{k+1}) $$\nPo indukcijski predpostavki je izraz v oklepaju večji od $(1+a_1+\\dots+a_k)$. Zato lahko zapišemo:\n$$ \\text{LHS} \u003e (1+a_1+a_2+\\dots+a_k)(1+a_{k+1}) $$\n\\textbf{Utemeljitev:} Neenakost se ohrani, ker množimo z $(1+a_{k+1})$, ki je zagotovo pozitiven (saj je $a_{k+1}\u003e0$).\n\nSedaj pomnožimo oklepaje na desni strani:\n$$ (1+a_1+\\dots+a_k)(1+a_{k+1}) \u003d (1+a_1+\\dots+a_k) \\cdot 1 + (1+a_1+\\dots+a_k) \\cdot a_{k+1} $$\n$$ \u003d (1+a_1+\\dots+a_k) + a_{k+1} + (a_1a_{k+1} + a_2a_{k+1} + \\dots + a_ka_{k+1}) $$\nTorej smo dobili:\n$$ \\text{LHS} \u003e (1+a_1+\\dots+a_k+a_{k+1}) + (a_1a_{k+1} + a_2a_{k+1} + \\dots + a_ka_{k+1}) $$\nPrimerjajmo ta izraz z desno stranjo, ki jo želimo dokazati ($1+a_1+\\dots+a_k+a_{k+1}$). Vidimo, da je naš izraz večji za vsoto produktov:\n$$ a_1a_{k+1} + a_2a_{k+1} + \\dots + a_ka_{k+1} $$\n\\textbf{Utemeljitev:} Ker so vsi $a_i$ pozitivni, je vsak produkt $a_ia_{k+1}$ pozitiven. Vsota pozitivnih števil je prav tako pozitivna. Torej velja:\n$$ (a_1a_{k+1} + a_2a_{k+1} + \\dots + a_ka_{k+1}) \u003e 0 $$\nS tem smo dokazali, da je:\n$$ \\text{LHS} \u003e (\\text{RHS za } n\u003dk+1) + (\\text{pozitivno število}) $$\nkar pomeni, da je zagotovo:\n$$ (1+a_1)(1+a_2)\\dots(1+a_{k+1}) \u003e 1+a_1+a_2+\\dots+a_k+a_{k+1} $$\nS tem je indukcijski korak dokazan.\n\n\\section*{Zaključek}\nKer veljata baza indukcije in indukcijski korak, je po principu matematične indukcije trditev dokazana za vsak $n \\in \\mathbb{N}, n \\ge 2$.\n\n\\hfill \\textit{Q.E.D.}\n\n\\end{document}\n```",

As you suspected, there\u0027s a lot of overlap, but your notes contain valuable derivations and problem examples that are crucial for understanding. I\u0027ve synthesized all of this into a new, structured file for your Obsidian vault, focusing on deriving the key equations and explaining the problem-solving strategies, just as you asked.\n\n---\n\n### File: `Fizika_I_Predavanje_1.md`\n\n# [[Predavanje 1: Uvod v Fiziko in Kinematika]]\n\nThis lecture provides an introduction to the Physics I course, outlines the fundamental SI units, and establishes the core concepts of kinematics—the mathematical description of motion.\n\n## [[Uvod v predmet (Course Introduction)]]\n\n### [[Vsebina (Content)]]\n*   **Fizika I:** [[Kinematika]], [[Dinamika]], [[Gravitacija]], [[Elastomehanika]], [[Mehanika tekočin]], [[Toplota]].\n*   **Fizika II:** [[Elektromagnetno polje]], [[Električni tok]], [[Nihanje]], [[Valovanje]], [[Osnove moderne fizike]], [[Fizikalni praktikum]].\n\n### [[Literatura (Literature)]]\n*   **A. Stanovnik:** *Fizika I in II* (Primary textbook)\n*   **D. Halliday, R. Resnick, Y. Walker:** *Fundamentals of Physics (HRW)* (Highly recommended)\n*   **J. Strnad:** *Fizika, 1. in 2. del*\n\n### [[Ocenjevanje (Grading System)]]\n*   **Fizika I:**\n    *   `40%` Pisni del (Written part - exam or two tests)\n    *   `60%` Ustni del (Oral part)\n    *   **Pogoj (Condition):** A passed written part is required to attend the oral exam.\n*   **Veljavnost pisnih testov (Validity of Written Tests):**\n    *   **Ocena $\\ge 50\\%$ (Grade $\\ge 50\\%$):** Valid for three consecutive exam periods.\n    *   **Ocena $40\\%-49\\%$ (Grade $40\\%-49\\%$):** Valid only for the first exam period immediately following the lectures (e.g., winter period for Physics I).\n\n---\n\n## [[Temeljni Pojmi Fizike (Fundamental Concepts of Physics)]]\n\nPhysics seeks to discover the fundamental laws of nature by finding relationships between measurable quantities.\n\n### [[Merske Enote in Napake (Units and Errors)]]\n*   **Količina (Quantity):** A physical property that can be measured (e.g., length, time, mass).\n*   **Merska Enota (Unit):** A standard used for comparison when measuring a quantity (e.g., meter, second, kilogram).\n*   **Napaka (Error):** Every measurement has an associated uncertainty.\n    *   **Absolutna napaka (Absolute Error):** Represents the range of uncertainty. E.g., $2.03 \\pm 0.04 \\, s$.\n    *   **Relativna napaka (Relative Error):** The error expressed as a fraction or percentage of the measured value. E.g., $2.03(1 \\pm 0.02) \\, s$, where $0.02$ is the $2\\%$ relative error.\n\n### [[Mednarodni Sistem Enot (The SI System)]]\nThe modern SI system (post-2019) defines its base units by fixing the values of fundamental physical constants. This ensures they are reproducible in any well-equipped lab.\n\n| Enota (Unit)     | Veličina (Quantity)        | Definicijska Konstanta (Defining Constant)                                                                         |\n| ---------------- | -------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n| **sekunda ($s$)**    | Čas (Time)                 | Frekvenca hiperfinega prehoda cezija-133 ($ \\Delta\\nu_{Cs} $)                                                          |\n| **meter ($m$)**      | Dolžina (Length)           | Hitrost svetlobe v vakuumu ($c$)                                                                                   |\n| **kilogram ($kg$)**  | Masa (Mass)                | Planckova konstanta ($h$)                                                                                          |\n| **amper ($A$)**      | Električni tok (Current) | Osnovni naboj ($e_0$)                                                                                               |\n| **kelvin ($K$)**     | Temperatura (Temperature)  | Boltzmannova konstanta ($k$)                                                                                       |\n| **mol ($mol$)**      | Množina snovi (Amount)     | Avogadrova konstanta ($N_A$)                                                                                       |\n| **kandela ($cd$)**   | Svetilnost (Luminosity)    | Spektralna svetlobna učinkovitost pri določeni frekvenci                                                          |\n\n---\n\n## [[Kinematika (Kinematics)]]\nKinematics is the description of motion without considering its causes (forces). We observe the object as a **točkasto telo (point mass)** and describe its motion within a chosen **opazovalni sistem (observational system)**, which consists of a coordinate system and a clock.\n\n### [[Premo Gibanje (1D Motion)]]\n*   **Lega (Position) $x$:** The location of an object on a coordinate axis.\n*   **Premik (Displacement) $\\Delta x$:** The change in position. It is a vector quantity (can be positive or negative).\n    $$ \\Delta x \u003d x_{končna} - x_{začetna} \u003d x - x\u0027 $$\n\n#### [[Hitrost (Velocity)]]\nVelocity describes the rate of change of position.\n\n*   **Povprečna hitrost (Average Velocity):** The total displacement divided by the time interval.\n    $$ \\bar{v} \u003d \\frac{\\Delta x}{\\Delta t} \u003d \\frac{x - x\u0027}{t - t\u0027} $$\n*   **Trenutna hitrost (Instantaneous Velocity):** The velocity at a specific moment in time.\n    *   **The \"Why\":** To find the velocity at an exact instant, we must make the time interval $\\Delta t$ infinitesimally small. This is the mathematical definition of a **derivative**. The instantaneous velocity is the first derivative of position with respect to time.\n    $$ v \u003d \\lim_{\\Delta t \\to 0} \\frac{\\Delta x}{\\Delta t} \u003d \\frac{dx}{dt} \u003d \\dot{x} $$\n    *   **Geometrijski Pomen:** The instantaneous velocity at any point on a position-time graph ($x-t$) is the **strmina (slope)** of the tangent line at that point.\n\n#### [[Pospesek (Acceleration)]]\nAcceleration describes the rate of change of velocity.\n\n*   **Povprečni pospešek (Average Acceleration):**\n    $$ \\bar{a} \u003d \\frac{\\Delta v}{\\Delta t} \u003d \\frac{v - v\u0027}{t - t\u0027} $$\n*   **Trenutni pospešek (Instantaneous Acceleration):** The acceleration at a specific moment. It is the first derivative of velocity with respect to time, and the *second derivative* of position with respect to time.\n    $$ a \u003d \\lim_{\\Delta t \\to 0} \\frac{\\Delta v}{\\Delta t} \u003d \\frac{dv}{dt} \u003d \\dot{v} \u003d \\frac{d}{dt}\\left(\\frac{dx}{dt}\\right) \u003d \\frac{d^2x}{dt^2} \u003d \\ddot{x} $$\n\n### [[Obratni Poti: Integral (The Reverse Path: The Integral)]]\nIf we know the acceleration, we can find the velocity by integration. If we know the velocity, we can find the position by integration.\n\n*   **From Acceleration to Velocity:** $\\Delta v \u003d \\int_{t\u0027}^{t} a(t) dt \\implies v(t) \u003d v\u0027 + \\int_{t\u0027}^{t} a(t) dt$\n*   **From Velocity to Position:** $\\Delta x \u003d \\int_{t\u0027}^{t} v(t) dt \\implies x(t) \u003d x\u0027 + \\int_{t\u0027}^{t} v(t) dt$\n*   **Geometrijski Pomen:** The displacement $\\Delta x$ between two points in time is the **ploščina (area)** under the velocity-time ($v-t$) graph between those two times.\n\n### [[Posebni Primeri Premočrtnega Gibanja (Special Cases of 1D Motion)]]\n\n#### [[Enakomerno Gibanje (Uniform Motion)]]\n*   **Conditions:** Velocity is constant ($v(t) \u003d v$), so acceleration is zero ($a\u003d0$).\n*   **Equations:**\n    $$ x(t) \u003d x\u0027 + v(t-t\u0027) $$\n\n#### [[Enakomerno Pospešeno Gibanje (Uniformly Accelerated Motion)]]\n*   **Conditions:** Acceleration is constant ($a(t) \u003d a$).\n*   **Key Equations:**\n\n| Formula                                                                               | Description                                    | Derivation / Application                                                                                                                              |\n| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| $v(t) \u003d v\u0027 + a(t-t\u0027)$                                                               | Velocity as a function of time                 | Direct integration of constant acceleration: $\\int a \\, dt \u003d at + C$.                                                                                 |\n| $x(t) \u003d x\u0027 + v\u0027(t-t\u0027) + \\frac{1}{2}a(t-t\u0027)^2$                                         | Position as a function of time                 | Integration of the velocity function: $\\int (v\u0027 + at) dt$.                                                                                          |\n| $v^2 \u003d v\u0027^2 + 2a(x-x\u0027)$                                                              | **\"Brezčasna enačba\" (Timeless Equation)** | **The \"Why\":** Derived by eliminating time. From the 1st eq: $t-t\u0027 \u003d \\frac{v-v\u0027}{a}$. Substitute this into the 2nd eq to get a direct link between velocity and position. |\n\n---\n\n## [[Vaje in Primeri (Exercises and Examples)]]\n\n### [[Primer: CD Zapis (CD Recording)]]\n**Naloga:** Zgoščenka ima 3 ure (3h) zvočnega zapisa. Dolžina sledi je $s\u003d5$ km. En bit podatkov zavzame $0.8 \\, \\mu m$ prostora. Koliko megabajtov (MB) podatkov je na zgoščenki?\n\n**Strategija in rešitev:**\n1.  **Pretvori enote (Convert Units):** All units must be consistent. Let\u0027s use micrometers ($\\mu m$).\n    $$ s \u003d 5 \\, \\text{km} \u003d 5 \\times 10^3 \\, \\text{m} \u003d 5 \\times 10^9 \\, \\mu m $$\n2.  **Izračunaj število bitov (Calculate Number of Bits):**\n    $$ N_{bit} \u003d \\frac{\\text{Skupna dolžina}}{\\text{Dolžina enega bita}} \u003d \\frac{5 \\times 10^9 \\, \\mu m}{0.8 \\, \\mu m/\\text{bit}} \u003d 6.25 \\times 10^9 \\, \\text{bitov} $$\n3.  **Pretvori v Bajte (Convert to Bytes):**\n    $$ 1 \\, \\text{Bait (Byte)} \u003d 8 \\, \\text{bitov} $$\n    $$ N_{B} \u003d \\frac{6.25 \\times 10^9}{8} \u003d 7.8125 \\times 10^8 \\, \\text{Baitov} $$\n4.  **Pretvori v Megabajte (Convert to Megabytes):**\n    *   **Note to Self:** In computer science, \"Mega\" usually means $2^{20}$ (Mebibyte, MiB), not $10^6$. The calculation in my notes uses this convention.\n    $$ N_{MiB} \u003d \\frac{N_B}{2^{20}} \u003d \\frac{7.8125 \\times 10^8}{1,048,576} \\approx 745 \\, \\text{MiB} $$\n    *   **Critical Analysis:** My handwritten calculation gave `781.25 MB`, which is what you get if you divide by $10^6$. Let\u0027s stick to the convention used in the notes for now, but be aware of the distinction between $10^6$ (MB) and $2^{20}$ (MiB). Let\u0027s re-calculate using the user\u0027s result: $N_{MB} \u003d \\frac{7.8125 \\times 10^8}{10^6} \u003d 781.25 \\, \\text{MB}$. This is likely what the lecturer intended.\n\n### [[DN: Blisk in Grom (Lightning and Thunder)]]\n**Naloga:** Strela udari v neznani oddaljenosti. Časovna razlika med tem, ko vidimo blisk in slišimo grom, je $\\Delta t \u003d 5 \\, s$. Koliko daleč je udarila strela?\n\n**Podatki (Given):**\n*   Hitrost zvoka (Speed of Sound): $c_{zv} \\approx 340 \\, m/s$\n*   Hitrost svetlobe (Speed of Light): $c_{sv} \u003d 3 \\times 10^8 \\, m/s$\n\n**Strategija in rešitev:**\n1.  **Model:** Let $d$ be the distance to the lightning strike.\n    *   Čas, ki ga potrebuje zvok: $t_{zv} \u003d \\frac{d}{c_{zv}}$\n    *   Čas, ki ga potrebuje svetloba: $t_{sv} \u003d \\frac{d}{c_{sv}}$\n2.  **Razmerje:** The observed time difference is:\n    $$ \\Delta t \u003d t_{zv} - t_{sv} \u003d 5 \\, s $$\n3.  **Poenostavitev (The \"Why\"):** The speed of light is enormously faster than the speed of sound. The time it takes for the light to reach us ($t_{sv}$) is practically zero compared to the time for sound.\n    $$ c_{sv} \\gg c_{zv} \\implies t_{sv} \\approx 0 $$\n    Therefore, we can approximate:\n    $$ \\Delta t \\approx t_{zv} $$\n4.  **Izračun (Calculation):**\n    $$ d \u003d c_{zv} \\cdot t_{zv} \\approx c_{zv} \\cdot \\Delta t $$\n    $$ d \\approx 340 \\, \\frac{m}{s} \\cdot 5 \\, s \u003d 1700 \\, m \u003d 1.7 \\, \\text{km} $$\n**Odgovor:** Strela je udarila približno 1.7 km stran.",

      }, {
        "text": "|\n\n---\n\n## [[Kinematika (Kinematics)]]\nKinematics is the description of motion without"
      }, {
        "text": " considering its causes (forces). We observe the object as a **točkasto telo (point mass)** and describe its motion within"
      }, {
        "text": " a chosen **opazovalni sistem (observational system)**, which consists of a coordinate system and a clock.\n\n### [[Premo Gibanje (1D Motion)]]\n*   **Lega (Position) $x$:** The location of an"
      }, {
        "text": " object on a coordinate axis.\n*   **Premik (Displacement) $\\Delta x$:** The change in position."
      }, {
        "text": " It is a vector quantity (can be positive or negative).\n    $$ \\Delta x \u003d x_{končna} - x_{za"
      }, {
        "text": "četna} \u003d x - x\u0027 $$\n\n#### [[Hitrost (Velocity)]]\nVelocity describes the rate of change of position.\n\n"
      }, {
        "text": "*   **Povprečna hitrost (Average Velocity):** The total displacement divided by the time interval.\n    $$ \\bar{"
      }, {
        "text": "v} \u003d \\frac{\\Delta x}{\\Delta t} \u003d \\frac{x - x\u0027}{t - t\u0027} $$\n*   **Tren"
      }, {
        "text": "utna hitrost (Instantaneous Velocity):** The velocity at a specific moment in time.\n    *   **The"
      }, {
        "text": " \"Why\":** To find the velocity at an exact instant, we must make the time interval $\\Delta t$ infinites"
      }, {
        "text": "imally small. This is the mathematical definition of a **derivative**. The instantaneous velocity is the first derivative of position with respect to time.\n    "
      }, {
        "text": "$$ v \u003d \\lim_{\\Delta t \\to 0} \\frac{\\Delta x}{\\Delta t} \u003d \\frac"
      }, {
        "text": "{dx}{dt} \u003d \\dot{x} $$\n    *   **Geometrijski Pomen:** The instantaneous"
      }, {
        "text": " velocity at any point on a position-time graph ($x-t$) is the **strmina (slope)** of the tangent"
      }, {
        "text": " line at that point.\n\n#### [[Pospesek (Acceleration)]]\nAcceleration describes the rate of change of velocity"
      }, {
        "text": ".\n\n*   **Povprečni pospešek (Average Acceleration):**\n    $$ \\bar{a} \u003d"
      }, {
        "text": " \\frac{\\Delta v}{\\Delta t} \u003d \\frac{v - v\u0027}{t - t\u0027} $$\n*   **Tren"
      }, {
        "text": "utni pospešek (Instantaneous Acceleration):** The acceleration at a specific moment. It is the first derivative of velocity"
      }, {
        "text": " with respect to time, and the *second derivative* of position with respect to time.\n    $$ a \u003d \\lim_{\\Delta t \\to"
      }, {
        "text": " 0} \\frac{\\Delta v}{\\Delta t} \u003d \\frac{dv}{dt} \u003d \\dot{"
      }, {
        "text": "v} \u003d \\frac{d}{dt}\\left(\\frac{dx}{dt}\\right) \u003d \\frac{d^2x}{dt^"
      }, {
        "text": "2} \u003d \\ddot{x} $$\n\n### [[Obratni Poti: Integral (The Reverse Path: The Integral)]]\nIf we know the acceleration, we can find the velocity by integration. If we know the velocity, we can find the position by"
      }, {
        "text": " integration.\n\n*   **From Acceleration to Velocity:** $\\Delta v \u003d \\int_{t\u0027}^{t} a(t) dt"
      }, {
        "text": " \\implies v(t) \u003d v\u0027 + \\int_{t\u0027}^{t} a(t) dt$"
      }, {
        "text": "\n*   **From Velocity to Position:** $\\Delta x \u003d \\int_{t\u0027}^{t} v(t) dt \\implies x("
      }, {

      }, {
        "text": " The displacement $\\Delta x$ between two points in time is the **ploščina (area)** under the velocity-time ($v-t$)"

      }, {
